<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Paraguay" title="Economy of Paraguay">Economy of Paraguay</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paraguay_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Paraguay_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/310px-Paraguay_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="310" height="202" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Paraguay_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/465px-Paraguay_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Paraguay_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/620px-Paraguay_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of Paraguay exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>For many years, the country's image was associated with the <a href="/wiki/Illicit_trade" title="Illicit trade">illicit trade</a> in <a href="/wiki/Electronics" title="Electronics">electronic products</a>, <a href="/wiki/Weapons" class="mw-redirect" title="Weapons">weapons</a> and <a href="/wiki/Drugs" class="mw-redirect" title="Drugs">drugs</a>. However, this scenario began to change in the 2000s, with the rise of legalized businesses such as the production of soy, maize, beef, among others. Data from the Central Bank of Paraguay (BCP) showed that, in 2006, exports related to the triangulation trade (China-Paraguay-Brazil), treated by Brazil – in most cases – as smuggling and embezzlement, represented 22% of the country's GDP. In 2016, this percentage dropped to 12%. With higher tax collections through legalized employment, the country has been able to improve its infrastructure, which was precarious. Paraguay has an economic development policy based on exports and dependence on the Brazilian market prevails. The triangulation trade has in its essence the export of products originating in China, with Brazil as its main destination. The energy exported is essentially the hydroelectric surplus generated by the Itaipu Power Plant, for which Brazil is the main buyer. And maquilas, whose main investors are Brazilians, also have a large consumer market in Brazil for their products. Therefore, in these three pillars of Paraguay's development strategy, there is the outstanding characteristic of re-exports. In the case of agricultural commodities, it is the export of products originating in Paraguay.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 2010s, the economy, largely directed towards soybean production, grew by an average of 4%. The economic growth did not, however, reduce poverty, which in 2018, according to official figures, reached more than 26% of the population. According to <i>The New York Times</i>, Paraguay is "one of the Latin American countries where the gap between rich and poor has widened the most in recent years." In the countryside, 85% of agricultural land is owned by 2.6% of the owners. In addition, people of indigenous descent have been expelled to make way for soybean companies.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Soybean" title="Soybean">soybean</a> export market is largely dominated by multinationals (<a href="/wiki/Cargill" title="Cargill">Cargill</a>, <a href="/wiki/Archer_Daniels_Midland" class="mw-redirect" title="Archer Daniels Midland">Archer Daniels Midland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bunge_Limited" title="Bunge Limited">Bunge Limited</a>, etc.) and therefore benefits few Paraguayans. Export companies and landowners pay little tax. For example, a study by <a href="/wiki/ECLAC" class="mw-redirect" title="ECLAC">ECLAC</a> (a UN agency) released in 2018 indicates that Paraguay is one of the countries where companies participate the least in the state budget.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pesticide" title="Pesticide">Pesticides</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Chemicals" class="mw-redirect" title="Chemicals">chemicals</a> are used heavily in soybean fields. The country's environmental standards are not met by companies and agrochemicals contaminate the environment. In 2019, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee" title="United Nations Human Rights Committee">United Nations Human Rights Committee</a> issued a ruling urging Paraguay to undertake an investigation into the massive fumigation of agrochemicals and the poisoning of the population. However, the situation is not improving: "The high level of political corruption, the extreme fragility of institutions, the high level of social vulnerability, the omnipotence of economic interests in political decisions, as well as the location [of the country] in the geographic heart of the Southern Cone and its abundance of natural resources, make Paraguay an attractive target for imperial geopolitical interests," says sociologist Tomás Palau.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Academic José Luis Insfrán also notes that the country's politicians "are subsidized by big business; they are the ones who implement the policies.
</p><p>In 2005, the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> stated that fewer than 10% of workers in Paraguay participate in the <a href="/wiki/Pension" title="Pension">pension</a> system, 95% of which is administered by two institutions. Both are financed on a <a href="/wiki/Pay-as-you-go_pension_plan" title="Pay-as-you-go pension plan">pay as you go</a> system by worker contributions; the first, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Instituto_de_Previsi%C3%B3n_Social&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Instituto de Previsión Social (page does not exist)">Instituto de Previsión Social</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instituto_de_Previsi%C3%B3n_Social_(Paraguay)" class="extiw" title="es:Instituto de Previsión Social (Paraguay)">es</a>&#93;</span> is for private sector employees, and the <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">caja fiscal</i></span> for public employees (including university professors, teachers, judicial employees, army officers and police officers) and veterans of the Chaco War (or their descendants).<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg/220px-Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg/330px-Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg/440px-Ciudad_de_San_Bernardino.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>As one of the most important <a href="/wiki/Resort_town" title="Resort town">resort towns</a> in Paraguay, <a href="/wiki/San_Bernardino,_Paraguay" title="San Bernardino, Paraguay">San Bernardino</a>, which was founded by German settlers in the 19th century, has one of the highest <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">purchasing power parities</a> in the country.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The market economy is distinguished by a large informal sector, featuring re-export of imported consumer goods to neighboring countries, as well as the activities of thousands of microenterprises and urban street vendors. Nonetheless, over the last 10 years the Paraguayan economy diversified dramatically, with the energy, auto parts and clothing industries leading the way.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Paraguay's most important <a href="/wiki/Urban_area" title="Urban area">urban areas</a> are located along the <a href="/wiki/Argentina-Paraguay_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Argentina-Paraguay border">Argentina-Paraguay border</a>: <a href="/wiki/Asunci%C3%B3n" title="Asunción">Asunción</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alberdi,_Paraguay" title="Alberdi, Paraguay">Alberdi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Encarnaci%C3%B3n,_Paraguay" title="Encarnación, Paraguay">Encarnación</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pilar,_Paraguay" title="Pilar, Paraguay">Pilar</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gran_Ciudad_del_Este" title="Gran Ciudad del Este">Ciudad del Este</a>, the latter being the third most important free commercial zone in the world, only trailing behind <a href="/wiki/Miami" title="Miami">Miami</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hong_Kong" title="Hong Kong">Hong Kong</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> A large percentage of the population, especially in rural areas, derives its living from agricultural activity, often on a subsistence basis. Because of the importance of the informal sector, accurate economic measures are difficult to obtain. The economy grew rapidly between 2003 and 2013 as growing world demand for commodities combined with high prices and favorable weather to support Paraguay's commodity-based export expansion.
</p><p>In 2012, Paraguay's government introduced the MERCOSUR (FOCEM) system in order to stimulate the economy and job growth through a partnership with both Brazil and Argentina.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The text near this tag is dated. (July 2021)">needs update</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Agriculture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Paraguay is the sixth-largest <a href="/wiki/Soybean" title="Soybean">soybean</a> producer in the world,<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> the second-largest producer of <a href="/wiki/Stevia" title="Stevia">stevia</a> and the ninth-largest exporter of <a href="/wiki/Beef" title="Beef">beef</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2018, in addition to soy, the country had a large production of <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">maize</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sugar_cane" class="mw-redirect" title="Sugar cane">sugar cane</a>, where it positioned itself as the 21st largest producer in the world; other important cultures of the country are <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rice" title="Rice">rice</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wheat" title="Wheat">wheat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Orange_(fruit)" title="Orange (fruit)">orange</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yerba_mate" title="Yerba mate">yerba mate</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sorghum" title="Sorghum">sorghum</a>. In livestock, Paraguay produced, in 2020, 481 thousand tons of beef, being the 26th largest producer in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The culture of soy was brought by Brazilians to the country: in 2019, almost 70% of soy and rice producers in Paraguay were people from Brazil, or descendants of Brazilians (the so-called brasiguaios). The first Brazilian producers began to arrive in the country in the 1980s. Before that, there was a lot of undisturbed land in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>More than 80% of the cultivable land is owned by 2.6% of landowners. Nearly 8&#160;million hectares were illegally granted, in violation of the agrarian law, to regime supporters during the dictatorship of Alfredo Stroessner (1954–1989), and the state has done nothing since to identify the beneficiaries of this illicit enrichment. These owners include generals, businessmen, and politicians, former presidents of the Republic, Nicaraguan dictator <a href="/wiki/Anastasio_Somoza_Debayle" title="Anastasio Somoza Debayle">Anastasio Somoza Debayle</a>, and even the ruling <a href="/wiki/Colorado_Party_(Paraguay)" title="Colorado Party (Paraguay)">Colorado Party</a>. More than 130&#160;peasant leaders have been assassinated since the fall of Stroessner in 1989.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-91">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The methods of appropriating land in Paraguay are numerous. Economist Luis Rojas says that the most common method is to buy land from small producers: "The peasant is offered a sum of money he has never seen in his life. He imagines it's a fortune, leaves for the city, spends it all in three or four months and makes the misery belts grow, because there is no work." Oxfam estimates that 900,000&#160;people have been evicted from the countryside in the last ten years, which represents nearly one-seventh of the population. This crowd of landless peasants is swelling the poverty belts around Asunción, the capital.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_91-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-91">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Industry_and_manufacturing">Industry and manufacturing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Industry and manufacturing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a> lists the top producing countries each year, based on the total value of production. By the 2019 list, Paraguay had the 79th&#160;most valuable industry in the world ($6.9 billion).<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The country was the seventh largest producer of <a href="/wiki/Soybean_oil" title="Soybean oil">soybean oil</a> in the world in 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Mineral_industry_of_Paraguay" title="Mineral industry of Paraguay">mineral industry of Paraguay</a> produces about 25% of the country's <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">gross domestic product</a> (GDP) and employs about 31% of the labor force. Production of <a href="/wiki/Cement" title="Cement">cement</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iron_ore" title="Iron ore">iron ore</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Steel" title="Steel">steel</a> occurs commonly throughout Paraguay's industrial sector. The growth of the industry was further fueled by the <a href="/wiki/Maquila" class="mw-redirect" title="Maquila">maquila</a> industry, with large industrial complexes located in the eastern part of the country. Paraguay put in place many incentives aimed to attract industries to the country. One of them is the so-called "Maquila law" by which companies can relocate to Paraguay, enjoying minimal tax rates.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Pharmaceutical_industry" title="Pharmaceutical industry">pharmaceutical industry</a>, Paraguayan companies now<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (February 2016)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> meet 70% of domestic consumption and have begun to export drugs. Paraguay is quickly<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="This term requires quantification. (February 2016)">quantify</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> supplanting foreign suppliers in meeting the country's drug needs.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Strong growth also is evident in the production of edible oils, garments, organic sugar, meat processing, and steel.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2003, manufacturing comprised 13.6% of the GDP and it employed about 11% of the working population in 2000. Paraguay's primary manufacturing focus is on food and beverages. Wood products, paper products, hides and furs, and non-metallic mineral products also contribute to manufacturing totals. Steady growth in the manufacturing GDP during the 1990s (1.2% annually) laid the foundation for 2002 and 2003, when the annual growth rate rose to 2.5%.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-96">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
Paraguay was ranked 88th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, up from 95th in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_issues_of_the_indigenous">Social issues of the indigenous</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Social issues of the indigenous">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Literacy rates have been extremely low among Paraguay's indigenous population, who had a literacy rate of 7.1% compared to the 51% rate of the general population as of the 2002 census.<sup id="cite_ref-paho_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-paho-101">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Only 2.5% of Paraguay's indigenous population had access to clean drinking water and only 9.5% had electricity as of 2002<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-paho_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-paho-101">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mapa_RutasPY2019.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Mapa_RutasPY2019.png/220px-Mapa_RutasPY2019.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="231" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Mapa_RutasPY2019.png/330px-Mapa_RutasPY2019.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Mapa_RutasPY2019.png/440px-Mapa_RutasPY2019.png 2x" data-file-width="1242" data-file-height="1305" /></a><figcaption>National roads of Paraguay.</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to official data from the M.O.P.C (Ministry of Public Works and Communications of Paraguay), in 2019, there were a total of 78,850&#160;km (48,995&#160;mi) of roads, of which 10,372&#160;km (6,445&#160;mi) were paved. <sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> One of the most important recent investments in the country's history is the construction of the <a href="/wiki/Route_15_(Paraguay)" title="Route 15 (Paraguay)">Bioceanic Corridor</a>, which will cross the north of Paraguay in a horizontal line, connecting Brazil to Argentina, reaching both the ports of northern Chile, as to Brazilian ports. The work will open a new route for exports of products to Asia, and will enable the development of an isolated region of Paraguay, the Chaco. In February 2022, Paraguay inaugurated 275 km of the road (about half of the route), connecting Carmelo Peralta (Alto Paraguay), on the border with Brazil, to Loma Plata (Boquerón), in the center of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Paraguay has also been implementing the country's first <a href="/wiki/Dual_carriageway" title="Dual carriageway">double highway</a>: 149&#160;km of the <a href="/wiki/Route_2_(Paraguay)" title="Route 2 (Paraguay)">Route&#160;2</a>, which connects the capital Asunción to Ciudad del Este, on the border with Brazil (the highway that also connects with <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Paranagu%C3%A1" title="Port of Paranaguá">port of Paranaguá</a>). In January&#160;2022, there were almost 100&#160;km duplicated.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Paran%C3%A1_River" title="Paraná River">Paraná-Paraguay system</a> is a natural north-south downstream waterway of 1,600&#160;km of a navigable network area, that gives the region direct access to the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">ocean</a>, therefore, river transport is important and extensive, concentrating the most commercial maritime traffic to the <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Buenos_Aires" title="Port of Buenos Aires">port of Buenos Aires</a>. Asunción is one of the main ports of Paraguay.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Silvio_Pettirossi_International_Airport" title="Silvio Pettirossi International Airport">Asunción airport</a> is an important stopover for international airlines and <a href="/wiki/Guaran%C3%AD_International_Airport" title="Guaraní International Airport">Ciudad del Este airport</a>, is an important international air cargo hub.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Energy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3><p>
All of <a href="/wiki/Electricity_sector_in_Paraguay" title="Electricity sector in Paraguay">Paraguay's electricity</a> is generated by <a href="/wiki/Hydroelectricity" title="Hydroelectricity">hydropower</a>, making it one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_electricity_production_from_renewable_sources" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by electricity production from renewable sources">cleanest in the world</a>. </p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg/220px-ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg/330px-ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg/440px-ItaipuAerea2AAL.jpg 2x" data-file-width="718" data-file-height="467" /></a><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
<p>Paraguay has an installed electrical production capacity of 8,110&#160;MW, producing 63&#160;billion <a href="/wiki/Kilowatt-hour" title="Kilowatt-hour">kWh</a>/year in 2016; with domestic consumption of just 15&#160;billion kWh, the excess production is sold to Brazil, Argentina, and Uruguay, making Paraguay the world's largest exporter of electric power.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> This production is from two large <a href="/wiki/Hydroelectric_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Hydroelectric power">hydroelectric power</a> projects along its borders, including the <a href="/wiki/Itaipu_Dam" title="Itaipu Dam">Itaipu Dam</a>, the world's second largest generating station.
</p><p><br />
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

    </div>
</div><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paraguay_population_density.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Paraguay_population_density.png/220px-Paraguay_population_density.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="176" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Paraguay_population_density.png/330px-Paraguay_population_density.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Paraguay_population_density.png/440px-Paraguay_population_density.png 2x" data-file-width="1030" data-file-height="823" /></a><figcaption>Paraguay population density (people per km<sup>2</sup>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Paraguay's population is distributed unevenly through the country, with the vast majority of people living in the eastern region near the capital and largest city, <a href="/wiki/Asunci%C3%B3n" title="Asunción">Asunción</a>, which accounts for 10% of the country's population. The <a href="/wiki/Gran_Chaco" title="Gran Chaco">Gran Chaco</a> region, which includes the <a href="/wiki/Alto_Paraguay_Department" title="Alto Paraguay Department">Alto Paraguay</a>, <a href="/wiki/Boquer%C3%B3n_Department" class="mw-redirect" title="Boquerón Department">Boquerón</a> and <a href="/wiki/Presidente_Hayes_Department" title="Presidente Hayes Department">Presidente Hayes Department</a>, and accounts for about 60% of the territory, is home to less than 4% of the population. About 63% of Paraguayans live in urban areas, making Paraguay one of the least urbanized nations in South America.
</p><p>For most of its history, Paraguay has been a recipient of immigrants, owing to its low population density, especially after the demographic collapse caused by the Paraguayan War. Immigrants include <a href="/wiki/Italians_in_Paraguay" class="mw-redirect" title="Italians in Paraguay">Italians</a>, Germans, Spanish, <a href="/wiki/English_people_in_Paraguay" title="English people in Paraguay">English</a>, Russians, <a href="/wiki/Koreans_in_Paraguay" title="Koreans in Paraguay">Koreans</a>, Chinese, <a href="/wiki/Lebanese_migration_to_Paraguay" class="mw-redirect" title="Lebanese migration to Paraguay">Arabs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Japanese_Paraguayans" title="Japanese Paraguayans">Japanese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ukrainians_in_Paraguay" title="Ukrainians in Paraguay">Ukrainians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Poles_(people)" class="mw-redirect" title="Poles (people)">Poles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jews" title="Jews">Jews</a>, Brazilians, <a href="/wiki/Argentines" title="Argentines">Argentines</a>, <a href="/wiki/Americans" title="Americans">Americans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bolivians" title="Bolivians">Bolivians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Venezuelans" title="Venezuelans">Venezuelans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mexicans" title="Mexicans">Mexicans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chileans" title="Chileans">Chileans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Taiwanese_people" title="Taiwanese people">Taiwanese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asian_people" title="Asian people">Asian people</a> and <a href="/wiki/Uruguayans" title="Uruguayans">Uruguayans</a>.
<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Along with <a href="/wiki/German_Argentines" title="German Argentines">German Argentines</a>, German Paraguayans are one of the most prominent and growing <a href="/wiki/Germans_in_Paraguay" class="mw-redirect" title="Germans in Paraguay">German</a> communities in South America, with some 25,000&#160;German-speaking <a href="/wiki/Mennonites_in_Paraguay" title="Mennonites in Paraguay">Mennonites</a> living in the Paraguayan <a href="/wiki/Gran_Chaco" title="Gran Chaco">Chaco</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> German settlers founded several towns, such as <a href="/wiki/Hohenau,_Paraguay" title="Hohenau, Paraguay">Hohenau</a>, <a href="/wiki/Filadelfia" title="Filadelfia">Filadelfia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Neuland_Colony" title="Neuland Colony">Neuland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Obligado" title="Obligado">Obligado</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nueva_Germania" title="Nueva Germania">Nueva Germania</a>. Several websites that promote German immigration to Paraguay claim that 5–7% of the population is of German ancestry,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement" title="Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute"><span title="The material near this tag is possibly inaccurate or nonfactual. (April 2014)">dubious</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:Paraguay#Dubious" title="Talk:Paraguay">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup> including 150,000&#160;people of German-Brazilian descent.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="Citations are from many German immigration promotion websites, there must be more objective sources for this (April 2014)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, from the <a href="/wiki/1920s" title="1920s">1920–30s</a>, Paraguay received <a href="https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inmigraci%C3%B3n_eslava_en_Paraguay" class="extiw" title="es:Inmigración eslava en Paraguay">waves</a> of <a href="/wiki/Slavs" title="Slavs">Slavic immigrants</a> who settled in Asunción and Southern Paraguay (<a href="/wiki/Itap%C3%BAa_Department" title="Itapúa Department">Itapúa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Misiones_Department" title="Misiones Department">Misiones</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C3%91eembuc%C3%BA_Department" title="Ñeembucú Department">Ñeembucú</a>), especially in the towns of <a href="/wiki/Fram,_Paraguay" title="Fram, Paraguay">Fram</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coronel_Bogado" title="Coronel Bogado">Coronel Bogado</a>, <a href="/wiki/Encarnaci%C3%B3n,_Paraguay" title="Encarnación, Paraguay">Encarnación</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Juan_del_Paran%C3%A1" title="San Juan del Paraná">San Juan del Paraná</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Ignacio,_Paraguay" title="San Ignacio, Paraguay">San Ignacio</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pilar,_Paraguay" title="Pilar, Paraguay">Pilar</a><sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> Paraguay has also been a haven for communities persecuted for the religious faith, like the <a href="/wiki/Bruderhof_Communities" title="Bruderhof Communities">Bruderhof</a> who were forced to leave England in 1941 because of their pacifist beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Many of these communities have retained their languages and culture, particularly the <a href="/wiki/Brazilian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Brazilian people">Brazilians</a>, who represent the largest and most prominent immigrant group, at around 400,000.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Many Brazilian Paraguayans are of German, Italian and Polish descent.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> There are an estimated 63,000&#160;<a href="/wiki/Afro-Paraguayans" title="Afro-Paraguayans">Afro-Paraguayans</a>, comprising 1% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources" title="Wikipedia:Reliable sources"><span title="The material near this tag may rely on an unreliable source. (April 2014)">unreliable source?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Caacupe5.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Caacupe5.jpg/220px-Caacupe5.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Caacupe5.jpg/330px-Caacupe5.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/bb/Caacupe5.jpg 2x" data-file-width="400" data-file-height="266" /></a><figcaption>A gathering in <a href="/wiki/Caacup%C3%A9" title="Caacupé">Caacupé</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>There is no official data on the ethnic composition of the Paraguayan population, as the Department of Statistics, Surveys and Censuses of Paraguay does not ask about <i>race</i> and <i>ethnicity</i> in census surveys, although it does inquire about the indigenous population.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2002&#160;census, indigenous people made up 1.7% of Paraguay's total population.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Traditionally, the majority of the Paraguayan population is considered mixed (<i>mestizo</i> in Spanish). According to the 2022 revision of the World Population Prospects<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-130">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-131">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>, Paraguay has a population of 6,703,799, of which 95% are Mestizo or white and 5% are labelled as "other", which includes members of 17&#160;distinct ethnolinguistic indigenous groups, many of which are poorly documented.
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1148739982">.mw-parser-output .largestCities-table-background{background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .largestCities-cell-background{background:#f0f0f0}</style>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Paraguay" title="Religion in Paraguay">Religion in Paraguay</a></div>
<p>Christianity, particularly <a href="/wiki/Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholicism">Catholicism</a>, is the dominant religion in Paraguay.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2002 census, 89.9% of the population was Catholic, 6.2% was <a href="/wiki/Evangelicalism" title="Evangelicalism">Evangelical Protestant</a>, 1.1% identified with other Christian sects, and 0.6% practiced indigenous religions. A U.S. State Department report on Religious Freedom names Catholicism, evangelical Protestantism, mainline Protestantism, Judaism (Orthodox, Conservative, and Reform), <a href="/wiki/Mormonism" title="Mormonism">Mormonism</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí Faith</a> as prominent religious groups. It also mentions a large Muslim community in <a href="/wiki/Alto_Paran%C3%A1" class="mw-redirect" title="Alto Paraná">Alto Paraná</a> (as a result of Middle-Eastern immigration, especially from <a href="/wiki/Lebanon" title="Lebanon">Lebanon</a>) and a prominent Mennonite community in Boquerón.<sup id="cite_ref-state1_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state1-134">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Paraguay" title="Languages of Paraguay">Languages of Paraguay</a></div>
<p>Paraguay is a bilingual nation. Both Spanish and <a href="/wiki/Guarani_language" title="Guarani language">Guaraní</a> are official languages. The Guaraní language is a remarkable trace of the indigenous Guaraní culture that has endured in Paraguay. Guaraní claims its place as one of the last surviving and thriving of South American indigenous national languages. In 2015, Spanish was spoken by about 87% of the population, while Guaraní is spoken by more than 90%, or slightly more than 5.8 million speakers. 52% of rural Paraguayans are bilingual in Guaraní. While Guaraní is still widely spoken, Spanish is generally given a preferential treatment in government, business, media and education as one of South America's <a href="/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca">lingua francas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ethnologue-list_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethnologue-list-135">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<p>There are approximately 19&#160;other indigenous languages spoken in Paraguay, many of which are threatened by extinction. Languages such as Guana, Ayoreo and Ishir (Chamacoco) are considered endangered.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Paraguay" title="Education in Paraguay">Education in Paraguay</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_universities_in_Paraguay" title="List of universities in Paraguay">List of universities in Paraguay</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_Paraguay" class="mw-redirect" title="List of high schools in Paraguay">List of high schools in Paraguay</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Literacy" title="Literacy">Literacy</a> was about 93.6% and 87.7% of Paraguayans finish the fifth grade according to <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>'s last Educational Development Index 2008. Literacy does not differ much by gender.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> A more recent study<sup id="cite_ref-abc.com.py_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abc.com.py-141">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> reveals that attendance at primary school by children between 6 and 12 years old is about 98%. Primary education is free and mandatory and takes nine years. Secondary education takes three years.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
Main universities in Paraguay include:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/National_University_of_Asunci%C3%B3n" class="mw-redirect" title="National University of Asunción">National University of Asunción</a> (public and founded in 1889)<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Autonomous_University_of_Asunci%C3%B3n" class="mw-redirect" title="Autonomous University of Asunción">Autonomous University of Asunción</a> (private and founded in 1979)<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Universidad_Cat%C3%B3lica_Nuestra_Se%C3%B1ora_de_la_Asunci%C3%B3n" class="mw-redirect" title="Universidad Católica Nuestra Señora de la Asunción">Universidad Católica Nuestra Señora de la Asunción</a> (private and run by the church).<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Universidad_Americana" class="mw-redirect" title="Universidad Americana">Universidad Americana</a> (private).</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Universidad_del_Pac%C3%ADfico_(Paraguay)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Universidad del Pacífico (Paraguay) (page does not exist)">Universidad del Pacífico</a> (private and founded in 1991).</li></ul>
<p>The net primary enrollment rate was at 88% in 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Public expenditure on education was about 4.3% of GDP in the early 2000s.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Paraguay" title="Health in Paraguay">Health in Paraguay</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg/220px-Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg/330px-Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg/440px-Life_expectancy_in_Paraguay.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Development of life expectancy</figcaption></figure>
<p>Average life expectancy in Paraguay is rather high given its poverty: as of 2006<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, it was 75&#160;years,<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> equivalent to far wealthier Argentina, and the eighth highest in the Americas according to World Health Organization. Public expenditure on health is 2.6% of <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">GDP</a>, while private health expenditure is 5.1%.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Infant mortality was 20 per 1,000&#160;births in 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Maternal mortality was 150 per 100,000&#160;live births in 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-hdrstats.undp.org_140-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdrstats.undp.org-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a> has helped the Paraguayan government reduce the country's maternal and infant mortality. The <i>Mother and Child Basic Health Insurance Project</i> aimed to contribute to reducing mortality by increasing the use of selected life-saving services included in the country's Mother and Child Basic Health Insurance Program (MCBI) by women of child-bearing age, and children under age six in selected areas. To this end, the project also targeted improving the quality and efficiency of the health service network within certain areas, in addition to increasing the Ministry of Public Health and Social Welfare's (MSPandBS) management.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Paraguay" title="Culture of Paraguay">Culture of Paraguay</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Paraguay" title="Music of Paraguay">Music of Paraguay</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Paraguay" title="Cinema of Paraguay">Cinema of Paraguay</a></div>
<p>Paraguay's cultural heritage can be traced to the extensive <a href="/wiki/Interracial_marriage" title="Interracial marriage">intermarriage</a> between the original male Spanish settlers and indigenous <a href="/wiki/Guarani_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Guarani people">Guaraní</a> women. Their culture is highly influenced by various European countries, including Spain. Therefore, the Paraguayan culture is a fusion of two cultures and traditions; one European, the other, Southern Guaraní. Although this is common throughout the country, it is more visible around the towns of <a href="/wiki/Atyr%C3%A1" title="Atyrá">Atyrá</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tobat%C3%AD" title="Tobatí">Tobatí</a> and <a href="/wiki/Altos,_Paraguay" title="Altos, Paraguay">Altos</a>. More than 93% of Paraguayans are <i><a href="/wiki/Mestizos" class="mw-redirect" title="Mestizos">mestizos</a></i>, making Paraguay one of the most homogeneous countries in Latin America. A characteristic of this cultural fusion is the extensive bilingualism present to this day: more than 80% of Paraguayans speak both Spanish and the indigenous language, <a href="/wiki/Guaran%C3%AD_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Guaraní language">Guaraní</a>. <a href="/wiki/Jopara" title="Jopara">Jopara</a>, a mixture of Guaraní and Spanish, is also widely spoken.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2014)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg/220px-Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg/330px-Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg/440px-Ovecha_Ragu%C3%A9_Festival_Paraguay.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3386" data-file-height="2640" /></a><figcaption>Ovecha Ragué Festival</figcaption></figure>
<p>This cultural fusion is expressed in arts such as embroidery (<i>ao po'í</i>) and <a href="/wiki/Nanduti" class="mw-redirect" title="Nanduti">lace making</a> (<i>ñandutí</i>). The <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Paraguay" title="Music of Paraguay">music of Paraguay</a>, which consists of lilting polkas, bouncy <i>galopas,</i> and languid <i><a href="/wiki/Guarania_(music)" title="Guarania (music)">guaranias</a></i> is played on the native harp. Paraguay's culinary heritage is also deeply influenced by this cultural fusion. Several popular dishes contain <a href="/wiki/Manioc" class="mw-redirect" title="Manioc">manioc</a>, a local staple crop similar to the <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">yuca</a> also known as Cassava root found in the <a href="/wiki/Southwestern_United_States" title="Southwestern United States">Southwestern United States</a> and Mexico, as well as other indigenous ingredients. A popular dish is <i><a href="/wiki/Sopa_paraguaya" title="Sopa paraguaya">sopa paraguaya</a></i>, similar to a thick <a href="/wiki/Corn_bread" class="mw-redirect" title="Corn bread">corn bread</a>. Another notable food is <i><a href="/wiki/Chipa" title="Chipa">chipa</a></i>, a <a href="/wiki/Bagel" title="Bagel">bagel</a>-like bread made from <a href="/wiki/Cornmeal" title="Cornmeal">cornmeal</a>, manioc, and cheese. Many other dishes consist of different kinds of cheeses, onions, bell peppers, cottage cheese, cornmeal, milk, seasonings, butter, eggs and fresh corn kernels.
</p><p>The 1950s and 1960s were the time of the birth of a new generation of Paraguayan novelists and poets such as <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Ricardo_Maz%C3%B3" title="José Ricardo Mazó">José Ricardo Mazó</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roque_Vallejos" title="Roque Vallejos">Roque Vallejos</a>, and Nobel Prize nominee <a href="/wiki/Augusto_Roa_Bastos" title="Augusto Roa Bastos">Augusto Roa Bastos</a>. Several <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Paraguay" title="Cinema of Paraguay">Paraguayan films</a> have been made.
</p><p>Inside the family, conservative values predominate. In lower classes, <a href="/wiki/Godparent" title="Godparent">godparents</a> have a special relationship to the family, since usually, they are chosen because of their favourable social position, in order to provide extra security for the children. Particular respect is owed them, in return for which the family can expect protection and patronage.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The most popular instruments in Paraguayan music are the harp and the guitar. The native genres are the Paraguayan polka and the guarania, characterised by a slow song that was developed by José Asunción Flores around the 1920s.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Paraguay" title="Sport in Paraguay">Sport in Paraguay</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg/220px-Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg/330px-Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg/440px-Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco_en_2019.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4608" data-file-height="3456" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Estadio_Defensores_del_Chaco" title="Estadio Defensores del Chaco">Estadio Defensores del Chaco</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Sport in Paraguay is an important part of the country's national culture. <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the most popular sport, the main national team is the <a href="/wiki/Paraguay_national_football_team" title="Paraguay national football team">Paraguay national football team</a>. 
<a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">Basketball</a> is also very popular.<sup id="cite_ref-Paraguay_in_Pictures_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Paraguay_in_Pictures-150">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Other sports such as <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Handball" title="Handball">handball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Futsal" title="Futsal">futsal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swimming_(sport)" title="Swimming (sport)">swimming</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tennis" title="Tennis">tennis</a> are also popular.<sup id="cite_ref-Paraguay_in_Pictures_150-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Paraguay_in_Pictures-150">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Additional Paraguayan sports and pastimes include <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">rugby union</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chess" title="Chess">chess</a>, <a href="/wiki/Motorsport" title="Motorsport">motorsport</a>, <a href="/wiki/Golf" title="Golf">golf</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rowing_(sport)" title="Rowing (sport)">rowing</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The reverse side of the National Seal of Paraguay:<br /><figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_(reverse).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg/100px-Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="100" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg/150px-Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg/200px-Coat_of_arms_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption></figcaption></figure><div style="clear:both;"></div> </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-2">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The reverse side of the Flag of Paraguay:<br /><figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_Paraguay_(reverse).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Flag_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg/100px-Flag_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="55" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Flag_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg/150px-Flag_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Flag_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg/200px-Flag_of_Paraguay_%28reverse%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="660" /></a><figcaption></figcaption></figure><div style="clear:both;"></div> </span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-30"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-30">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">
The Blanco Party of Uruguay, hardline right wing and reactionary at those days, was in the Uruguayan Government during the outbreak of the war and were allies of the Paraguayan Government.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-32"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-32">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">
<a href="/wiki/Richard_Francis_Burton" title="Richard Francis Burton">Burton</a>, a witness of the conflict, marks this date (12–16 October 1864) as the real beginning of the war. He writes (and it's the most logic account, considering the facts): <i>The Brazilian Army invades the <a href="/wiki/Banda_Oriental" title="Banda Oriental">Banda Oriental</a>, despite the protestations of President López, who declared that such invasion would be held a "casus belli"</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-35"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-35">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">
The classical view asserts that Francisco Solano López's expansionist and hegemonic views are the main reason for the outbreak of the conflict. The traditional Paraguayan view, held by the "<i>lopistas</i>" (supporters of Solano López in Paraguay and elsewhere), holds that Paraguay acted in self-defense and for the protection of the equilibrium of the Plate Basin. This view is usually contested by the "<i>anti-lopistas</i>" (known in Paraguay as "<i>legionarios</i>"), who favored the "Triple Alliance." Revisionist views from right and left national populists put a great emphasis on the influence of the British Empire, a view that a majority of historians reject.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-38"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-38">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">
Some of the documents taken by Brasil during the war, were returned to Paraguay in the collection known as "Colección de Río Branco", nowadays in the National Archives of Asunción, Paraguay.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Paraguay&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
